### GatewayCorrelationMiddleware

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Gateway` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Gateway/GatewayCorrelationMiddleware.cs:30` · Level 9 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: Middleware that guarantees every request entering the edge carries an `X-Correlation-ID`, writing the value onto the **request** headers when the caller supplied none or an unusable one (over 64 characters is cut, non-printable-ASCII is replaced), and echoing it back on the response.
- **Depends on**: `RequestDelegate`, `HttpContext` and `System.Diagnostics.Activity` only. Registered through [GatewayCorrelationExtensions](#gatewaycorrelationextensions). It is the edge twin of [CorrelationIdMiddleware](group-12-api-hosting-mapping.md#correlationidmiddleware) in `MMCA.Common.API`.
- **Concept introduced, ensuring an id rather than reading one.** `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`, `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`. A correlation id minted independently by each service is not a correlation id: a browser call crossing the gateway into two services produces two unrelated ids, neither of them present in the gateway's own logs, and the one process guaranteed to see the request exactly once is the only one not stamping it. The fix is not "log an id at the edge" but "write the id onto the outbound request", because the service-side middleware adopts a header that is already present instead of minting its own (doc comment `:19-25`). **[ADR-088](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/088-gateway-edge-responsibilities.html)** records this as the first of the three responsibilities the edge owns, extending [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html)'s correlation id one hop outward.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - `HeaderName = "X-Correlation-ID"` (`:36`), a `public const`. Its doc comment (`:31-35`) flags that this is deliberately the *same literal* as `CorrelationIdMiddleware.HeaderName` in `MMCA.Common.API` rather than a shared reference: the two packages share no dependency, and the whole point of the pair is that the edge and the services agree on the string. (Compare [Extensions](#extensions-1), which mirrors two configuration section names for the same reason and pins the duplication with a test.)
  - `MaxLength = 64` (`:43`, `internal const`, doc comment `:38-42`) is the longest correlation id kept. It is the twin of `CorrelationIdMiddleware.MaxLength` in `MMCA.Common.API` (the persisted column width), repeated as a literal on purpose for the same no-shared-reference reason as `HeaderName`.
  - `InvokeAsync(HttpContext)` (`:51`) null-guards the context (`:53`) and reads the first inbound value of the header into `header` (`:55`).
  - The id is then computed in one expression (`:62-64`): a non-blank inbound value passes through `Sanitize` (`:87`), and when that yields null (blank, or rejected) the id falls back to `Activity.Current?.TraceId` and then to `context.TraceIdentifier` (the same precedence the service middleware uses, so the correlation id and the distributed trace line up). The comment (`:57-61`) says why the rule is mirrored: the response echo runs from `OnStarting` after the forwarder copied the service's headers, so the edge must forward and echo exactly what the service keeps and stores.
  - **Write-back is conditional on a change.** When the computed id differs from the inbound header (`:66`), it is **written back onto `context.Request.Headers`** (`:68`), which covers a minted id, a truncated one and a replaced one. That single assignment is what makes the forwarded request carry the id downstream.
  - `Sanitize(string)` (`:87`, private static, doc comment `:81-86`) cuts the value to `MaxLength` characters (`:89`) and keeps it only when every character is printable ASCII, `>= ' '` and `<= '~'` (`:90`); otherwise it returns null so a generated id replaces it.
  - The response echo is registered through `context.Response.OnStarting` (`:72-76`) rather than set inline, because a proxied response has its headers written by the forwarder; a callback fired as the response starts survives that. Finally the pipeline continues by returning `next(context)` directly (`:78`): `InvokeAsync` is a plain `Task`-returning method, not `async` (`:51`), so there is no state machine and no `await` on the hop, which is safe because nothing runs after the downstream call completes.
- **Why it's built this way**: `sealed`, conventional (constructor plus `InvokeAsync`) middleware, and **context-free by constraint, not by accident**. Its only constructor dependency is the `RequestDelegate` (`:29`). The service-tier version writes the id onto a scoped `ICorrelationContext` that the CQRS logging decorators read, and this Aspire package cannot reference the Application layer that declares that abstraction. The doc comment states the constraint directly (`:10-17`): a reverse-proxy gateway forwards bytes, it has no application container and no correlation context to populate, which is what makes this middleware safe to drop into a bare YARP host with no DI graph beyond the proxy.
- **Where it's used**: Both gateway pipelines, deliberately first among each gateway's own concerns so the id is stamped before anything (a 429 from the edge limiter included) can short-circuit: `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:188` and `MMCA.Store/Source/Hosts/MMCA.Store.Gateway/Program.cs:155`. Covered by [GatewayCorrelationMiddlewareTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), and end to end by [GatewayHardeningTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) in both apps.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: A caller-supplied id is bounded and shaped (at most 64 printable-ASCII characters) but is otherwise client-controlled, so a client still chooses the value that appears in gateway and service logs. The `64` and the ASCII rule are mirrored by hand from `CorrelationIdMiddleware` in `MMCA.Common.API`; nothing in this file ties the two copies together.

---

### GatewayCorrelationExtensions

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Gateway` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Gateway/GatewayCorrelationMiddleware.cs:100` · Level 10 · class (static)

- **What it is**: The one-line pipeline registration for [GatewayCorrelationMiddleware](#gatewaycorrelationmiddleware): `app.UseGatewayCorrelation()`.
- **Depends on**: [GatewayCorrelationMiddleware](#gatewaycorrelationmiddleware) and `IApplicationBuilder` (ASP.NET Core).
- **Concept reinforced**: the `Use*` pipeline-extension idiom. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`, `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: the value this adds over calling `UseMiddleware<T>()` directly is the **documented ordering rule**, which is the part a host gets wrong.
- **Walkthrough**: One `extension(IApplicationBuilder app)` block (`:75`) containing `UseGatewayCorrelation()` (`:82`), which null-guards the receiver (`:84`) and returns `app.UseMiddleware<GatewayCorrelationMiddleware>()` (`:85`). The doc comment carries the contract (`:77-79`): call it **first**, before the proxy or forwarder is mapped, so the id is on the request the gateway forwards. The class carries the same scoped `CA1708` suppression as its siblings (`:69-72`) for the `extension(T)` analyzer false positive.
- **Why it's built this way**: A static class with an `extension(T)` block is the codebase's standard registration idiom (see [primer, C# `extension(T)` types](00-primer.md#c-extensiont-types-read-this-once), and [ADR-106](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/106-extension-members-as-public-di-surface.html) for the policy). Keeping the extension in the same file as the middleware means the ordering rule and the behaviour it protects are read together.
- **Where it's used**: `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:188` and `MMCA.Store/Source/Hosts/MMCA.Store.Gateway/Program.cs:155`, in both cases before every other gateway concern, and in particular before [GatewayRateLimitingExtensions](#gatewayratelimitingextensions)' `UseGatewayRateLimiting()` (ADC `:144`, Store `:170`), so a throttled request still carries a correlation id.

### Extensions

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.cs:23` · Level 3 · class (static)

> Disambiguation: this is the **framework** (Common.Aspire) `Extensions`, the canonical service-defaults bootstrap consumed by every running service, including all four ADC services and all three Store services (neither app ships a local `ServiceDefaults` project). The other class named `Extensions` in this chapter is the [Common.Aspire.Hosting `Extensions`](#extensions-1), the AppHost-side broker, JWKS, and data-source wiring.

- **What it is**: The shared Aspire service-defaults bootstrap: `AddServiceDefaults()`, `AddWarmupReadiness()`, `ConfigureOpenTelemetry()`, `AddDefaultHealthChecks()`, `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks()`, `AddWarmupTask<T>()`, `MapDefaultEndpoints()`, and `MapCachedHealthChecks()`. It configures OpenTelemetry (logs, metrics, tracing), Kestrel with no `Server` response header, cached health-check endpoints (`/health`, `/alive`, `/health/ready`), service discovery, the warm-up readiness pipeline, a startup tripwire for vendor integrations configured but never registered, and a Polly resilience pipeline plus a FinOps-tuned `SocketsHttpHandler` for every outbound `HttpClient`.
- **Depends on**: [IWarmupTask](#iwarmuptask), [OpenIdConnectMetadataWarmupTask](#openidconnectmetadatawarmuptask), [WarmupHostedService](#warmuphostedservice), [WarmupReadinessGate](#warmupreadinessgate), [WarmupReadinessHealthCheck](#warmupreadinesshealthcheck), [HealthCheckTags](#healthchecktags), [HealthEndpointPaths](#healthendpointpaths), [RedisPingHealthCheck](#redispinghealthcheck), [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider), [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions), [OutboxPollFilterProcessor](#outboxpollfilterprocessor), [ProbeTelemetryFilter](#probetelemetryfilter), [ProbeTelemetryFilterProcessor](#probetelemetryfilterprocessor), [HttpResilienceDefaults](#httpresiliencedefaults), [HostIntegrationTripwire](#hostintegrationtripwire), [HostIntegrationTripwireOptions](#hostintegrationtripwireoptions), and [RetryAfterHeader](#retryafterheader); plus Azure Monitor, OpenTelemetry, Polly / `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience`, the AspNetCore health-check packages, and the RabbitMQ client (NuGet).
- **Concept introduced, Aspire service defaults as a shared cross-cutting bootstrap.** `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` (centralised OpenTelemetry plus health endpoints; it subscribes eight MMCA meters, Polly's own resilience meter, and the `MMCA.Common.Outbox`/`MMCA.Common.InternalCommands` trace sources by literal name, plus, new, the optional AI package's meter and trace source under the single literal `AiTelemetryName = "MMCA.Common.AI"` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:43`), inert until a host adopts `MMCA.Common.AI` and sets `Ai:Enabled`, and installs [OutboxPollFilterProcessor](#outboxpollfilterprocessor) to drop noisy idle-poll spans before export, plus, by default, the two probe-telemetry filters). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` (Polly on every outbound client, [ADR-009](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/009-resilience-and-recovery-objectives.html), and the Polly meter now exported so a retry storm or an open circuit shows up as a signal rather than plain latency). `[Rubric §31, Cost / FinOps]` (four trace/metric-side cost knobs: `Telemetry:FilterProbeTelemetry` on by default, `Telemetry:EnablePollyDurationMetrics` off by default, plus `Telemetry:TracesSampleRatio` and the two HttpClient/runtime metric-family switches opt-in; and the `SocketsHttpHandler` tuning at `Extensions.cs:107-115`, whose rationale comment at `:96-106` is the codebase's clearest FinOps decision: `PooledConnectionLifetime` picks up ACA replica DNS rollover, `PooledConnectionIdleTimeout` avoids repeated TLS handshakes, and socket keep-alive pings keep TCP alive **without counting as ACA user traffic**, so the replica stays on idle-vCPU billing, roughly 8x cheaper than active). `[Rubric §27, Security/AppSec]` (the health-report cache: SEC-Common-71/SEC-ADC-17 bound an anonymous-flood amplification on `/health` and `/health/ready`, see the `MapCachedHealthChecks` bullet below). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` (one call, one baseline, every host). The telemetry half of this is **[ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html)**; the warm-up half is **[ADR-025](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/025-startup-warmup-readiness.html)**.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - `AddServiceDefaults<TBuilder>()` (`Extensions.cs:35`, in a generic `extension<TBuilder>(TBuilder builder) where TBuilder : IHostApplicationBuilder` block at `:25-26`, so it works for a web host or a worker): calls `ConfigureOpenTelemetry`, `AddDefaultHealthChecks`, `AddWarmupReadiness`, then `AddServiceDiscovery` (`:37-40`). It then turns off Kestrel's `Server` response header with `Configure<KestrelServerOptions>(kestrel => kestrel.AddServerHeader = false)` (`:44`); the comment (`:42-43`) gives the reason, the header names the server family to a scanner and carries nothing a client needs. It then registers the startup tripwire (`:50-52`): `AddOptions<HostIntegrationTripwireOptions>().ValidateOnStart()` plus a `TryAddEnumerable` of [HostIntegrationTripwire](#hostintegrationtripwire) as the `IValidateOptions<HostIntegrationTripwireOptions>`. The comment above it (`:46-49`) names what it catches, a vendor integration the configuration asks for but no adapter call registered (an Application Insights connection string with no exporter, a vault URI with no configuration source, a key-ring blob with no DataProtection sink), and when: with the other `ValidateOnStart` validators, before any hosted service starts. Next comes `ConfigureHttpClientDefaults` (`:57`), which affects **every** `HttpClient` registered downstream through the client factory (typed clients, named clients, and the gateway's own downstream probe clients, which opt out of the resilience handler again, see [GatewayHealthCheckExtensions](#gatewayhealthcheckextensions)). The YARP forwarder is not one of them: it builds its clients through its own forwarder client factory (comment `:54-56`). Inside it, `AddStandardResilienceHandler` (`:68`) pulls all four values from [HttpResilienceDefaults](#httpresiliencedefaults) (`:70-73`): a 30-second per-attempt timeout, a 60-second circuit-breaker sampling window, a 90-second total request timeout, and `MaxRetryAttempts = 1`. It then calls `options.Retry.DisableFor(HttpMethod.Post, HttpMethod.Patch)` (`:82`), so a POST or PATCH is never replayed: the comment at `:75-81` explains that an attempt which timed out may still be running server-side and most hops carry no idempotency key (a server-to-server call never does; the UI's `APIClient` stamps one only on `EntityServiceBase` creates and updates, whose replay the UI service base classes own), while GET, PUT, DELETE and the other idempotent verbs keep their one retry, which is why it names the two verbs instead of using `DisableForUnsafeHttpMethods` (that helper would also switch the retry off for PUT and DELETE). It then wraps the retry's `ShouldHandle` (`:88-92`): when [RetryAfterHeader](#retryafterheader)`.ExceedsRetryBudget(args.Outcome.Result, TimeProvider.System)` is true the outcome is not retried, otherwise the original predicate decides. The comment at `:84-87` gives the reason: `ShouldRetryAfterHeader` (on by default) makes the retry wait out a 429's `Retry-After`, which a rate limiter sets to the rest of its window, up to a minute holding the request; a 429 asking for more than `HttpResilienceDefaults.MaxRetryAfterWait` (8 seconds, `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Shared/Resilience/HttpResilienceDefaults.cs:40`) is therefore not retried here, matching the UI service base classes, so a refused action is one request. `http.AddServiceDiscovery()` (`:94`) lets clients resolve logical service names, and `ConfigurePrimaryHttpMessageHandler` installs the tuned `SocketsHttpHandler` (`:107-115`) including `KeepAlivePingPolicy.WithActiveRequests` and `EnableMultipleHttp2Connections = true` (`:113-114`, so one multiplexed HTTP/2 connection cannot become a bottleneck). The comment at `:59-67` is worth reading for the retry-budget reasoning: exactly **one** retry per hop, because the UI service base classes own user-facing retries, and full budgets at both layers would multiply a backend brownout into up to sixteen gateway hits per user action. The same comment notes that the gRPC typed clients in `MMCA.Common.Grpc` share only the retry budget and the breaker window from [HttpResilienceDefaults](#httpresiliencedefaults), not the timeouts.
  - `AddWarmupReadiness()` (`Extensions.cs:130`): registers the singleton [WarmupReadinessGate](#warmupreadinessgate) (`:132`), the [WarmupHostedService](#warmuphostedservice) runner (`:133`), the built-in [OpenIdConnectMetadataWarmupTask](#openidconnectmetadatawarmuptask) as an `IWarmupTask` (`:135`), which pre-fetches the OIDC discovery document for the configured `Authentication:JwtBearer:Authority` (doc comment `:121-128`), and the [WarmupReadinessHealthCheck](#warmupreadinesshealthcheck) as the `"warmup"` check tagged `HealthCheckTags.Ready` (`:137-138`). `AddWarmupTask<TTask>()` (`:153`, in an `extension(IServiceCollection services)` block at `:144`) lets a consumer add service-specific warm-ups by registering `AddSingleton<IWarmupTask, TTask>()` (`:156`).
  - `ConfigureOpenTelemetry()` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:70`): logging with `IncludeFormattedMessage` and `IncludeScopes` (`:161-162`); the metrics half is now a dedicated private `ConfigureMetrics(metrics, configuration)` method (`:166`, defined at `:520`, "a method rather than an inline lambda so each knob stays readable"). Tracing adds the application's own source plus `MMCA.Common.Outbox`, `MMCA.Common.InternalCommands`, and, new, `.AddSource(AiTelemetryName)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:86`), the optional AI package's combined trace/metric name: inert until a host both references `MMCA.Common.AI` and enables it, because subscribing a source nothing publishes to is a no-op. Then branches on the probe-telemetry cost knob, `IsProbeTelemetryFilterEnabled` (`:199`). When filtering is on (the default), ASP.NET Core and `HttpClient` instrumentation are added *with* filters, `options.Filter = Telemetry.ProbeTelemetryFilter.ShouldCollectRequest` and `options.FilterHttpRequestMessage = Telemetry.ProbeTelemetryFilter.ShouldCollectOutgoing` (`:191-194`); when it is off they are added plain (`:198-199`). The comment above the branch (`:173-184`) carries the measurement behind it: Container Apps liveness and readiness probes, the gateway's downstream aggregate probes, YARP active health checks and the availability web test made up 100% of the `AppRequests` rows in both production workspaces, and their children (the health check's `SELECT 1`, the Redis PING, the gateway's calls to each backend's `/alive`) most of the `AppDependencies` rows; sampling cannot help there, because probe spans are exactly what the sampler keeps proportionally. It also records what is deliberately left alone: metrics, so probe traffic still shows on dashboards. Because both filters configure the **default-named** instrumentation options, they also apply to the instrumentation the Azure Monitor distro adds, which is why this knob needs no View, unlike the metrics knobs in `ConfigureMetrics`. `.AddProcessor(new Telemetry.OutboxPollFilterProcessor())` (`:207`) always registers, and a `ProbeTelemetryFilterProcessor` only under the same knob, both before the exporters so their `OnEnd` clears the `Recorded` flag first. When `TryGetTraceSampleRatio` succeeds it installs `new ParentBasedSampler(new TraceIdRatioBasedSampler(ratio))`, parent-based so a sampled-in request keeps its whole trace intact across service boundaries. Finally it calls the private `AddOpenTelemetryExporters` (`:334`).
  - `ConfigureMetrics(metrics, configuration)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:240`, private static): always adds ASP.NET Core instrumentation (`:522`), then the two metric-family **disable** knobs, `Telemetry:DisableHttpClientMetrics` (`:531`) and `Telemetry:DisableRuntimeMetrics` (`:558`), each a two-part switch where the disabled branch does not merely skip the instrumentation call: it installs a `metrics.AddView(...)` that returns `MetricStreamConfiguration.Drop` for the `System.Net.Http`/`System.Net.NameResolution` meters (`:543-547`) or the `System.Runtime` meter (`:563-566`). The comment above the HttpClient branch (`:524-530`) records why the View is needed: a deployed host also calls `UseAzureMonitor()`, and the Azure Monitor distro adds those meters itself, so the toggle is authoritative only because a View applies to the whole `MeterProvider` regardless of which component added the meter. It then subscribes **eight** MMCA meters by literal name (`:582-589`): `MMCA.Common.Outbox`, `.Cqrs`, `.Idempotency`, `.Scheduler`, `.Broker`, `.OutputCache`, `.BestEffort`, and `.InternalCommands`, plus, new, a ninth `.AddMeter(AiTelemetryName)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:320`) for the optional AI package's token spend and call duration, inert until a host adds that package and enables `Ai:Enabled`. The comment above them (`:573-581`) names what each carries and which are inert until a host opts in. It then adds Polly's own meter, `PollyMeterName = "Polly"` (`:49`, `:596`), the meter `Polly.Extensions`' telemetry listener emits through, named as a literal because Aspire holds no reference to Polly's own assemblies (`:47-48`); the comment at `:591-595` explains why this matters: the standard resilience handler is on every `HttpClient` and every gRPC typed client, so this meter is the only production signal that a client is retrying, timing out, or being refused by an open circuit, and until it was subscribed a brownout looked, from the outside, exactly like latency. The always-exported counter is `resilience.polly.strategy.events` (`PollyStrategyEventsInstrument`, `:57`), tagged with `pipeline.name`, `strategy.name`, `event.name` (OnRetry/OnCircuitOpened/OnCircuitClosed/OnTimeout/...), `event.severity` and `exception.type` (`:52-56`). Polly's two duration **histograms**, `resilience.polly.strategy.attempt.duration` and `resilience.polly.pipeline.duration` (`PollyAttemptDurationInstrument`/`PollyPipelineDurationInstrument`, `:60,63`), are the new opt-in cost knob: dropped by a `metrics.AddView` (`:608-613`) unless a host sets `Telemetry:EnablePollyDurationMetrics=true` (`EnablePollyDurationMetricsConfigKey`, `:43`), because they re-measure what `http.client.request.duration` and the dependency traces already report and run on a pipeline invoked on every outbound call (`:598-605`).
  - `TryGetTraceSampleRatio(configuration, out ratio)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:182`, `internal static`): reads the optional `Telemetry:TracesSampleRatio` knob and returns `true` only for a value that parses invariantly and falls in the open interval (0,1); absent, unparseable, or out-of-range input returns `false` and leaves `ratio` at `1.0`. Sampling therefore fails toward keeping everything, so a typo can never silently drop *all* telemetry.
  - `IsInstrumentationDisabled(configuration, configKey)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:205`, `internal static`): the fail-safe shape for the two metric-family **disable** knobs, `bool.TryParse(configuration[configKey], out var disabled) && disabled`. It returns `true` (drop that family) only when the value parses as boolean `true`; absent, blank, or unparseable keeps the instrumentation.
  - `IsInstrumentationEnabled(configuration, configKey)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:218`, `internal static`) is the new mirror image for the Polly duration histograms, an opt-**in** knob: `bool.TryParse(configuration[configKey], out var enabled) && enabled`. Absent, blank, or unparseable all mean "stay off", so a typo can never silently add a high-volume stream to a host's bill; this is the opposite bias from `IsInstrumentationDisabled`, where absent/unparseable keeps the (already-on) instrumentation.
  - `IsProbeTelemetryFilterEnabled(configuration)` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:230`, `internal static`) reads the key held as `FilterProbeTelemetryConfigKey = "Telemetry:FilterProbeTelemetry"` (`:35`) and inverts the default: `!bool.TryParse(...) || enabled`, so absent, blank, or unparseable all mean "filter" and only an explicit `false` turns filtering off. That is the opposite bias from the metrics disable knobs, and the doc comment says why: probe chatter is ingestion no host wants billed, while a metrics family dropped by accident is a blind spot.
  - `AddDefaultHealthChecks()` (`Extensions.Health.cs:25`) registers a `"self"` check tagged `HealthCheckTags.Live` (`:239-240`). New (SEC-Common-71, SEC-ADC-17, `:242-244`): it also binds and validates [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions) (`:245-248`), and registers `TimeProvider.System` (`:249`) plus a singleton [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider) (`:250`), the short-TTL single-flight report cache that `MapCachedHealthChecks` serves `/health` and `/health/ready` from.
  - `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks(bool requireDatabase = false)` (`Extensions.Health.cs:76`) is the conditional part. It resolves the health-checks builder (`:283`), delegates the relational engines to the private `AddDatabaseHealthChecks` (`:285`), then adds Redis and RabbitMQ only when their connection strings are present. The Redis branch (`:287-304`) is the incident-hardened one: it `TryAddSingleton`s a [RedisPingHealthCheck](#redispinghealthcheck) built from the connection string (`:296-297`) and registers it as a `HealthCheckRegistration` named `"redis"` tagged `[HealthCheckTags.Optional]` (`:299-303`). The comment above it (`:290-295`) explains both halves: PING only, never an administrative command, because the `AspNetCore.HealthChecks.Redis` check this replaced issued `CLUSTER INFO` against any server StackExchange.Redis 3.x detects as clustered (which is how it sees Azure Managed Redis Enterprise tier) and the server refuses that command outside admin mode, so every probe threw against a healthy cache; and singleton registration so the fallback multiplexer is built once, not per probe, and disposed with the container. The RabbitMQ branch (`:306-316`) reads the `rabbitmq` connection string falling back to `messaging` (`:306-307`), requires it to parse as an absolute URI (`:308-309`), and registers an `AddRabbitMQ` check named `"rabbitmq"` tagged `[HealthCheckTags.Optional]` (`:311-315`) whose factory builds a connection per probe. The parameter doc (`:262-266`) now names a third accepted shape: `SQLServerConnectionString`, **`PostgreSQLConnectionString`**, or `SqliteConnectionString`.
  - `AddDatabaseHealthChecks(healthChecks, configuration, requireDatabase)` (`Extensions.Health.cs:237`, private static) holds the asymmetry that matters. It now collects SQL Server, **PostgreSQL**, and SQLite sources (`:635-637`), and when `requireDatabase` is true and **all three** are empty it throws `InvalidOperationException` naming all three accepted shapes (`:639-645`). Otherwise it adds one `AddSqlServer` check per SQL source (`:647-650`), one `AddNpgSql` check per PostgreSQL source (`:652-655`, new), and one `AddSqlite` check per SQLite source (`:657-660`), all **untagged**, so every one of them gates readiness. The reasoning is unchanged: Redis and RabbitMQ are optional per host, but a host that cannot resolve its own database connection string is misconfigured, and silently registering no check would let it report healthy and take traffic it cannot serve.
  - `RelationalSources(configuration, key)` (`Extensions.Health.cs:326`, private static) is the small piece of logic behind "one check per database", now driving three engines instead of two: it maps `key` to `sqlserver`/`postgresql`/`sqlite` (`:682-687`), reads the top-level `ConnectionStrings` entry (`:691-695`) and then every named `DataSources` child carrying the same key (`:697-704`), and finally deduplicates **by connection string** with an ordinal `HashSet` (`:706-714`). Two details are deliberate: the first surviving database keeps the historical check name for its engine and only a genuinely second, different database gets the `{engine}:{source}` form; and because deduplication is by connection string, logical names that collapse onto one physical database contribute one check rather than one per logical name.
  - `MapDefaultEndpoints()` (`Extensions.Health.cs:143`, in an `extension(WebApplication app)` block at `:110`): `/health` now maps through `MapCachedHealthChecks(HealthEndpointPaths.Health, predicate: null)` (`:395`) instead of `MapHealthChecks` directly; `/alive` still filters to `HealthCheckTags.Live` via plain `MapHealthChecks`, but now with an explicit `.AllowAnonymous()` (`:399-402`, new SECURITY comment at `:386-388`: a host that adopts the framework's fallback authorization policy would otherwise 401 its own liveness/readiness probes, and a probe that cannot answer takes the replica out of rotation); `/health/ready` also now goes through `MapCachedHealthChecks`, with a `static` predicate excluding `Live` and `Optional` tags (`:415-417`). All three routes still come from the [HealthEndpointPaths](#healthendpointpaths) constants.
  - `MapCachedHealthChecks(path, predicate)` (`Extensions.Health.cs:192`, new public method) is the security fix behind the `/health` and `/health/ready` rewrite (SEC-Common-71, SEC-ADC-17, comment at `:390-393`): it maps GET/HEAD to a handler that calls `CachedHealthReportProvider.GetReportAsync(path, predicate, cancellationToken)` (`:437-441`) instead of running the underlying checks per request, then answers exactly as `MapHealthChecks` would, `text/plain` with the status name, `503` only when unhealthy (`:443-450`). The endpoint is `.AllowAnonymous().ExcludeFromDescription()` (`:452-453`). Because the cache is keyed by `path`, an anonymous flood on either cached endpoint costs at most one probe round per cache window rather than one per request; `/alive` is deliberately left uncached (mapped with plain `MapHealthChecks`) since it is a single in-memory check with no downstream dependency to protect.
  - `AddOpenTelemetryExporters()` (`Extensions.Telemetry.cs:164`, private): enables **OTLP** when `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is set (`:336-342`, the local Aspire dashboard sets it) and **Azure Monitor** when `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` is set (`:344-350`, set by the Container Apps Bicep). Both can be active simultaneously, and each exporter ships only its own copy.
- **Why it's built this way**: One bootstrap means a baseline change (a new meter, a tighter timeout, the poll filter, the Redis check swap, the health-report cache) propagates to every consumer in lockstep. There is a single framework copy and no per-app variants: neither ADC nor Store ships an app-local `ServiceDefaults` project, so the warm-up gate, the MMCA meters, the poll filter, and the Azure Monitor exporter branch are uniform across every service. Every knob that could hide **end-user** signal defaults to the expensive-but-safe setting, which is the deliberate bias recorded in [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html): a cost surprise is recoverable, a silent telemetry blackout during an incident is not. The two knobs that default the other way, `Telemetry:FilterProbeTelemetry` and `Telemetry:EnablePollyDurationMetrics`, are the exceptions that prove the rule: what the first drops is the platform probing itself, and what the second withholds is a histogram that duplicates signal already captured elsewhere; neither one hides an incident. The health-report cache added under `AddDefaultHealthChecks`/`MapCachedHealthChecks` extends the same bias to a different axis, DoS resistance: an anonymous flood on `/health` or `/health/ready` can no longer force one dependency probe round per request. The class also carries a scoped `CA1708` suppression documenting a known analyzer false positive: with two or more `extension(T)` blocks in one static class, the compiler-generated grouping members trip the "identifiers should differ by more than case" rule.
- **Where it's used**: `builder.AddServiceDefaults()` early in each framework-consuming host's `Program.cs`: ADC Gateway (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:66`), ADC UI (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:54`), the four ADC services (Identity `:97`, Conference `:100`, Engagement `:83`, Notification `:86`), the Store gateway, UI and three services (`MMCA.Store/Source/Hosts/MMCA.Store.Gateway/Program.cs:65`, `MMCA.Store/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.Store.UI.Web/Program.cs:76`, `MMCA.Store/Source/Services/MMCA.Store.Identity.Service/Program.cs:74`, `MMCA.Store/Source/Services/MMCA.Store.Catalog.Service/Program.cs:68`, `MMCA.Store/Source/Services/MMCA.Store.Sales.Service/Program.cs:85`), and the Helpdesk seed (`MMCA.Helpdesk/Source/Hosts/MMCA.Helpdesk.Web/Program.cs:14`). `app.MapDefaultEndpoints()` follows `Build()` in each (ADC Gateway `:136`, ADC UI `:119`, ADC Conference `:375`, Helpdesk `:129`). `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks(requireDatabase: true)` is the standard service-host call: ADC Identity `:146`, Conference `:163`, Engagement `:143`, Notification `:128`, Store Identity `:129`, Catalog `:133`, Sales `:142`, and Helpdesk `:31`. The internal cost-knob helpers are covered by [TracesSampleRatioTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) and [MetricsInstrumentationToggleTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup); the Polly meter by [PollyResilienceMetricsTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Telemetry/PollyResilienceMetricsTests.cs`); the conditional infrastructure checks by [InfrastructureHealthChecksTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) and [RedisReadinessSafetyTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: Because the POST/PATCH retry exclusion lives in `ConfigureHttpClientDefaults`, it applies to every `HttpClient` in the host; a transient failure on a non-idempotent call surfaces after one attempt, and any replay for those verbs is the caller's decision. The same host-wide scope applies to the 429 rule: a 429 whose `Retry-After` exceeds `MaxRetryAfterWait` reaches the caller at once on every verb, and a 429 without the header follows the normal one-retry, no-POST/PATCH rule. `requireDatabase` defaults to `false`, so a service host that forgets to pass `true` registers no database check and can report ready without one; every current service host passes it. The RabbitMQ branch silently registers nothing when the connection string is present but not a valid absolute URI, so a malformed broker URI produces no check and no error at startup. Probe-trace filtering is on by default, so a host debugging its own probes sees no request traces for them until it sets `Telemetry:FilterProbeTelemetry=false`; nothing logs that the filter is active. `Telemetry:EnablePollyDurationMetrics` defaults to off, so the per-attempt and per-pipeline duration histograms are silent unless a host explicitly opts in. The `AiTelemetryName` source and meter are always subscribed regardless of whether a host references `MMCA.Common.AI`; nothing logs that they are inert, and `AiDependencyIsolationTestsBase` is what keeps the reference itself out of hosts that never adopt the package. `/health` and `/health/ready` now serve a cached report rather than always running the underlying checks live; the cache window/behavior is governed by [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions) and [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider), not by this class.

### Entry

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/CachedHealthReportProvider.cs:33` · Level 0 · class (private, sealed, nested)

- **What it is**: The per-endpoint-key cache slot nested inside [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider): the last computed `HealthReport`, when it expires, and the single-flight semaphore that keeps concurrent callers for the same key from all recomputing it at once.
- **Depends on**: `HealthReport` (ASP.NET Core diagnostics), `SemaphoreSlim`, `DateTimeOffset` (BCL). Owned exclusively by [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider), which keys one instance per `endpointKey` in its `_entries` dictionary (`CachedHealthReportProvider.cs:30`).
- **Walkthrough**: `Report` (`:35`) is nullable and mutable, holding the last computed report or `null` before the first probe. `ExpiresAt` (`:37`) is the `DateTimeOffset` the owner advances after each recompute. `Refresh` (`:39`) is a `SemaphoreSlim(1, 1)`, the single-flight gate: only one caller per key is ever inside `HealthCheckService.CheckHealthAsync` at a time (see [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider)'s `GetReportAsync`). `Dispose()` (`:41`) releases that semaphore.
- **Why it's built this way**: `private sealed`, an implementation detail of one class with no meaning outside it. `IDisposable` so the owner's own `Dispose()` (`CachedHealthReportProvider.cs:142-153`), which iterates `_entries.Values`, can release every semaphore it created without a separate tracking list.
- **Where it's used**: Created and looked up by `CachedHealthReportProvider.GetEntry` (`CachedHealthReportProvider.cs:111-120`) under the owner's `_gate` lock, read by `TryReadFresh` (`:125-129`) and `ReadAny` (`:133-137`), and disposed in bulk from `CachedHealthReportProvider.Dispose` (`:142-153`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: Not determinable from source: entries are never evicted for endpoint keys that stop being probed, so `_entries` only grows for the life of the provider.

### HealthReportCacheOptions

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/HealthReportCacheOptions.cs:29` · Level 0 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: The bound `"HealthChecks"` configuration for the health endpoints, with two settings: how many seconds [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider) reuses a computed health report, and how many seconds each infrastructure dependency check may run before it is cancelled.
- **Depends on**: `System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations` (`[Range]`) only. Consumed by [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider) via `IOptions<HealthReportCacheOptions>`, and read directly from configuration by `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` in the [Common.Aspire Extensions](#extensions-1) for the per-check timeout.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Two `internal const` bounds, `MinimumCheckTimeoutSeconds = 1` (`:32`) and `MaximumCheckTimeoutSeconds = 60` (`:35`), shared by the `[Range]` attribute and the registration-time check so the two cannot disagree.
  - `SectionName = "HealthChecks"` (`:38`), a `static readonly string` used for options binding.
  - `CacheSeconds` (`:45`, `[Range(0, 300)]` at `:44`) defaults to **5**. The doc comment (`:40-43`) states the escape hatch: `0` disables caching and restores the probe-per-request behaviour.
  - `CheckTimeoutSeconds` (`:55`, `[Range(MinimumCheckTimeoutSeconds, MaximumCheckTimeoutSeconds)]` at `:54`) defaults to **5**. Its doc comment (`:47-53`) states the effect: a dependency that holds a connection without answering turns `/health` unhealthy within `CacheSeconds` plus this value instead of leaving the last report in place, and a host whose dependency legitimately answers slower raises it.
- **Why it's built this way**: Two `int` settings rather than a richer policy, because the only knobs a cached health report needs are how stale a stand-in answer may be and how long one probe may hang; single-flight recomputation and per-key isolation are [CachedHealthReportProvider](#cachedhealthreportprovider)'s fixed behaviour, not configuration. The timeout is validated twice on purpose: `AddDefaultHealthChecks` binds the section with `ValidateDataAnnotations().ValidateOnStart()` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.Health.cs:33-36`), but `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` needs the value while it registers checks, before any options validation runs, so its `CheckTimeout` helper re-reads the section and throws `InvalidOperationException` outside 1 to 60 (`Extensions.Health.cs:294-304`). Part of the same edge caching work **[ADR-088](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/088-gateway-edge-responsibilities.html)** records for the gateway.
- **Where it's used**: Bound in `AddDefaultHealthChecks` (`Extensions.Health.cs:33-36`, beside the `TryAddSingleton<CachedHealthReportProvider>()` at `:38`), read by `CachedHealthReportProvider.GetReportAsync` on every call (`CachedHealthReportProvider.cs:59`), and read once at registration by `CheckTimeout` (`Extensions.Health.cs:296-298`), whose result becomes the `timeout:` of every database, Redis and RabbitMQ registration (`:100`, `:123`, `:260`, `:277`, `:283`). Covered by `CachedHealthReportProviderTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Health/CachedHealthReportProviderTests.cs`, 6 references).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `CheckTimeoutSeconds` bounds only the checks `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` registers; the `"self"` liveness check and any check a host adds itself carry no timeout from this type. Not determinable from source: whether `/health` and `/health/ready` share one cache window or are keyed independently is decided by the `endpointKey` value each caller passes to `GetReportAsync`, not by this type.

### PostgreSqlHealthCheck

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/PostgreSqlHealthCheck.cs:26` · Level 0 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: The framework's readiness check for one PostgreSQL database. It opens a connection and runs `SELECT 1` under the probe's cancellation token, on the **same** `NpgsqlDataSource` the PostgreSQL contexts use whenever the host registered one (class doc `PostgreSqlHealthCheck.cs:7-10`).
- **Depends on**: `NpgsqlDataSource` (Npgsql), `IHealthCheck` / `HealthCheckResult` (ASP.NET Core diagnostics), `IKeyedServiceProvider`, and the `Lock` type. The keyed data source it looks for is registered by `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure`; the engine itself is covered at **[ADR-113](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/113-postgresql-as-a-first-class-engine.html)**. Registered by the [Common.Aspire Extensions](#extensions-1).
- **Concept introduced, a probe must authenticate the way the workload does.** `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`, `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`, `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]`. Infrastructure registers the data source as a keyed singleton, keyed by the connection string, once a data-source configurator is added (RDS IAM tokens through `AddAwsRdsIamAuthentication`, for one). A check built from the bare connection string would then connect without the password provider and report a healthy database as down (`:12-15`). This package cannot reference Infrastructure, so the contract is an Npgsql type plus a string, decided **per probe** because the two registrations can arrive in either order (`:16-17`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Five fields (`:28-32`): the connection string, the provider, a `Lock`, the nullable `_ownedDataSource`, and a `_disposed` flag. The constructor (`:37-44`) guards both arguments and does no I/O.
  - `CheckHealthAsync` (`:47`) opens a connection from `DataSourceForProbe()` (`:55`), runs `SELECT 1;` (`:61-62`), and returns `Healthy` (`:66`). `OperationCanceledException` **when the probe token was cancelled** is rethrown (`:68-71`); every other exception becomes a result with the registration's `FailureStatus` and the description `"PostgreSQL did not answer SELECT 1."` (`:73-80`).
  - `DataSourceForProbe()` (`:102-103`, `internal` so the tests can see the choice) returns `SharedDataSource() ?? OwnedDataSource()`. `SharedDataSource` (`:105-108`) asks an `IKeyedServiceProvider` for the `NpgsqlDataSource` keyed by the connection string. `OwnedDataSource` (`:110-117`) builds one with `NpgsqlDataSource.Create` on first use under the lock and throws `ObjectDisposedException` once the check is disposed (`:114`).
  - `Dispose` (`:84`) and `DisposeAsync` (`:87-94`) both go through `TakeOwnedDataSourceForDisposal` (`:119-128`), which sets the flag and hands out the owned data source exactly once; the shared one is never disposed here, since the container that registered it owns it (`:22-23`).
- **Why it's built this way**: Decided on every call rather than at construction, because the keyed data source can be registered after this check. The owned fallback is kept for every later probe so its pool carries the reuse (`:20-22`) instead of a new data source per probe. `IDisposable` plus `IAsyncDisposable`, because the check is a container singleton and the owned data source must be released with it.
- **Where it's used**: Registered once per PostgreSQL source in the database loop of `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.Health.cs:263-278`): a keyed singleton keyed by the check name (`:269-271`) resolved by the `HealthCheckRegistration` (`:272-277`) with no tags and the default failure status, so it gates readiness (`:255-256`), and with the per-check timeout from [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions). Covered by `PostgreSqlHealthCheckDataSourceTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Health/PostgreSqlHealthCheckDataSourceTests.cs`, including `DisposingTheContainer_DisposesTheOwnedDataSource_ButNotTheSharedOne` at `:62`) and `DependencyHealthCheckTests.PostgreSql_WhenTheServerIsUnreachable_ReportsTheFailureStatus` (`DependencyHealthCheckTests.cs:75`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: A `PostgreSqlHealthCheck` resolved through a provider that is not an `IKeyedServiceProvider` always uses its owned data source. No consumer AppHost wires a PostgreSQL source today (see [Extensions](#extensions-1)), so this path runs only where a host configures one.

### RabbitMqHealthConnection

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/RabbitMqHealthConnection.cs:22` · Level 0 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: The holder of the one AMQP connection the [RabbitMqHealthCheck](#rabbitmqhealthcheck) probes through. It hands out the held connection while it is open and replaces it only when it is gone or closed (class doc `RabbitMqHealthConnection.cs:5-20`).
- **Depends on**: `RabbitMQ.Client` (`IConnection`, `ConnectionFactory`), `TimeProvider.System`, and the `Lock` type. Used only by [RabbitMqHealthCheck](#rabbitmqhealthcheck); registered as a singleton by the [Common.Aspire Extensions](#extensions-1).
- **Concept introduced, a probe must not leak what it opens.** `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`, `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`, `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`. The check asks for a connection on every probe round and never disposes it, so opening one per call would leave one AMQP connection on the broker for every successful round (`:8-10`). The holder keeps at most one for the whole process. The second half of the concept is a bounded handshake: a broker port that accepts TCP and never answers must end the attempt on the holder's own timeout, even when no probe is still waiting (`:12-14`). **[ADR-009](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/009-resilience-and-recovery-objectives.html)** is the governing resilience record.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Fields (`:24-34`): a `Func<CancellationToken, Task<IConnection>>` creation delegate, the timeout, a `Lock`, the held `_connection`, the in-flight `_creating` task, and a `_disposed` flag. The public constructor (`:39-42`) builds the delegate from a `ConnectionFactory { Uri = uri }`; the `internal` one (`:47-54`) takes any delegate, which is what the tests drive, and rejects a non-positive timeout (`:50`).
  - `GetAsync` (`:64-89`), under the lock: throw `ObjectDisposedException` once disposed (`:69`), return an open held connection directly (`:71-74`), otherwise start a single creation if none is in flight (`:76-83`), taking the stale connection out of the holder first so no later caller can be handed it (`:78-81`). Every caller then waits on that one task with its own token (`:88`), so a caller's cancellation stops only that caller.
  - `CreateAsync` (`:194-224`) disposes the stale connection best effort, connects, and stores the result only if the holder was not disposed meanwhile (`:205-212`); otherwise it disposes the new connection and throws `ObjectDisposedException` (`:214-215`). Its `finally` clears `_creating` (`:217-223`), so a failed creation is not cached and the next round tries again.
  - `ConnectWithinTimeoutAsync` (`:166-192`) gives the attempt a `CancellationTokenSource` cancelled at the timeout (`:168`) and also abandons the wait at the timeout through `WaitAsync(_timeout, TimeProvider.System)` (`:174`), for a client that ignores its token. On `TimeoutException` it cancels the token explicitly (`:183`) and hands the abandoned task to `DisposeWhenCreatedAsync` (`:184`, `:144-159`), which disposes any late connection and only then the token source.
  - `DisposeAsync` (`:92-112`) is idempotent, sets the flag, and disposes the held connection best effort; a creation still in flight sees the flag and disposes its own result (`:107`).
- **Why it's built this way**: Single flight because ten concurrent probe rounds must not open ten connections. A creation token owned by the holder rather than the probe's token, because the handshake must end even after every waiting probe has given up. The best-effort helpers (`DisposeQuietlyAsync` `:115-125`, `CancelQuietlyAsync` `:128-138`) catch everything except `OutOfMemoryException`, because a connection that fails to close is already of no use and a throwing cancel callback must not replace the timeout being reported.
- **Where it's used**: Registered with `TryAddSingleton(_ => new Health.RabbitMqHealthConnection(rabbitUri, checkTimeout))` in `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.Health.cs:116`), with the per-check timeout from [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions) as its creation budget; the comment above it (`:108-115`) states the design. Covered by `RabbitMqHealthConnectionTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Health/RabbitMqHealthConnectionTests.cs`), whose cases include `TenConcurrentFirstCalls_CreateExactlyOneConnection` (`:77`), `ACreationThatNeverCompletes_EndsAtTheTimeout_AndLeavesNothingHeld` (`:124`) and `ACreationThatFinishesAfterDispose_IsDisposed_AndNotHeld` (`:109`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: An attempt abandoned at the timeout keeps running in the background until the client returns; `DisposeWhenCreatedAsync` is fire-and-forget (`:184`), so nothing awaits that cleanup at host shutdown. Not determinable from source: how long RabbitMQ.Client takes to honour a cancelled handshake token.

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/SqliteHealthCheck.cs:15` · Level 0 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: The framework's readiness check for one SQLite database: open a connection, run `SELECT 1`, both under the probe's cancellation token (class doc `SqliteHealthCheck.cs:6-14`).
- **Depends on**: `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` (`SqliteConnection`) and `IHealthCheck` / `HealthCheckResult` (ASP.NET Core diagnostics). Registered by the [Common.Aspire Extensions](#extensions-1).
- **Walkthrough**: The constructor (`:21-25`) guards and stores the connection string and does no I/O. `CheckHealthAsync` (`:28`) creates a `SqliteConnection` (`:36`), opens it (`:39`), runs `SELECT 1;` (`:44-45`) and returns `Healthy` (`:49`). A cancellation **of the probe token** is rethrown (`:51-54`); any other exception becomes a result with the registration's `FailureStatus` and the description `"SQLite did not answer SELECT 1."` (`:56-63`).
- **Why it's built this way**: Stateless, because the provider's pool, keyed by the connection string, already carries connection reuse (`:10-12`), so one instance is safely shared across probes. The rethrow filter is the same one every dependency check in this folder uses: host shutdown is not reported as a database fault. It mirrors [SqlServerHealthCheck](#sqlserverhealthcheck) line for line apart from the driver.
- **Where it's used**: Constructed once per SQLite source in the database loop of `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.Health.cs:280-284`) and registered with no tags, so it gates readiness (`:255-256`), and with the per-check timeout from [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions) (`:283`). Covered by `DependencyHealthCheckTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Health/DependencyHealthCheckTests.cs:88`, `:98`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: Opening a SQLite connection to a missing file creates it unless the connection string sets a read-only or open-existing mode, so for a default connection string this check proves the file is openable rather than that the schema exists. Not determinable from source: which modes consumer connection strings use.

### CachedHealthReportProvider

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/CachedHealthReportProvider.cs:25` · Level 1 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: A short-lived, per-endpoint-key cache in front of `HealthCheckService.CheckHealthAsync`, with single-flight recomputation so a burst of concurrent probes against the same endpoint triggers at most one round of health checks.
- **Depends on**: [Entry](#entry) (its cache slot), [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions) (its configuration), `HealthCheckService`, `HealthReport`, `HealthCheckRegistration` (ASP.NET Core diagnostics), and `TimeProvider` (BCL, injected rather than read from `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` so the cache window is testable).
- **Concept introduced, single-flight caching for a probe endpoint.** `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`, `[Rubric §29, Resilience]`, `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`. §12 assesses whether a hot path avoids redundant work under load; a health endpoint polled every few seconds by both Container Apps and a load balancer is exactly that hot path, and without a cache each poll re-runs every registered `IHealthCheck`, including whatever they do over the network. The comment on `GetReportAsync`'s wait logic (`:73-74`, `:83`) states the resilience half directly: a caller that cannot take the refresh slot serves the stale report rather than queuing behind the in-flight probe, so a slow dependency never converts a probe flood into a request backlog. **[ADR-088](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/088-gateway-edge-responsibilities.html)** is the governing record.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Two fields (`:30-31`): `_entries`, a `Dictionary<string, Entry>` keyed by `endpointKey`, and `_gate`, a `Lock` guarding all reads and writes of that dictionary and of an [Entry](#entry)'s `Report`/`ExpiresAt` pair.
  - `GetReportAsync(endpointKey, predicate, cancellationToken)` (`:52`) null-guards `endpointKey` (`:57`), then reads `HealthReportCacheOptions.CacheSeconds` into a `window` (`:59`). When `window <= TimeSpan.Zero` it skips the cache entirely and calls `CheckHealthAsync` directly (`:60-63`), so `CacheSeconds = 0` genuinely restores the pre-caching probe-per-request behaviour.
  - Otherwise it fetches the [Entry](#entry) for the key (`:65`) and returns immediately if `TryReadFresh` (`:125-129`) finds an unexpired report (`:68-71`).
  - The single-flight gate: `entry.Refresh.WaitAsync(TimeSpan.Zero, ...)` (`:75`) tries to take the slot without blocking. A caller that loses the race serves whatever is cached, even if stale, through `ReadAny` (`:77-81`, `:133-137`); only when nothing has ever been cached for this key does it fall back to a blocking `WaitAsync` (`:84`), because there is no stand-in answer to serve.
  - Inside the `try` (`:87-104`) it re-checks freshness once more (`:90-93`), since the holder it waited behind may have just published a report, then calls `CheckHealthAsync` (`:95`) and stores the result under `_gate` (`:97-101`) before returning it. The `finally` (`:105-108`) always releases `Refresh`.
  - `GetEntry` (`:111`), `TryReadFresh` (`:125`) and `ReadAny` (`:133`) are all `private` and take the lock internally, so `GetReportAsync` never holds `_gate` across an `await`.
  - `Dispose()` (`:142`) takes `_gate`, disposes every [Entry](#entry), and clears the dictionary.
- **Why it's built this way**: `TimeProvider` injected rather than read from the clock directly, the standard testability seam for time-based caches in this codebase. `Lock` (the .NET 9+ type) rather than a plain `object` for `_gate`, guarding only dictionary and field access, never an `await`, which is why the semaphore (not the lock) is what serializes the actual recompute. The non-blocking-first, blocking-fallback shape on `Refresh` is what lets a cache miss degrade to "serve stale" under load instead of "every caller queues behind one probe".
- **Where it's used**: Constructed and injected via `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.cs` (3 references) and consumed by ADC's gateway health endpoints (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs`, 1 reference). Covered by `CachedHealthReportProviderTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Health/CachedHealthReportProviderTests.cs`, 2 references).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `_entries` has no eviction and no cap: nothing bounds it, provided the set of `endpointKey` values callers pass stays small and fixed. Not determinable from source: the exact set of `endpointKey` values in use; that is a decision at each call site, not this type.

### RabbitMqHealthCheck

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/RabbitMqHealthCheck.cs:15` · Level 1 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: The framework's RabbitMQ reachability check. It takes the connection [RabbitMqHealthConnection](#rabbitmqhealthconnection) holds and opens, then closes, one channel on it under the probe's cancellation token (class doc `RabbitMqHealthCheck.cs:5-14`).
- **Depends on**: [RabbitMqHealthConnection](#rabbitmqhealthconnection) (the only connection it ever uses), `RabbitMQ.Client` (`IConnection.CreateChannelAsync`), and `IHealthCheck` / `HealthCheckResult` (ASP.NET Core diagnostics). Registered by the [Common.Aspire Extensions](#extensions-1) under the [HealthCheckTags](#healthchecktags) `Optional` tag.
- **Walkthrough**: The constructor (`:21-25`) guards and stores the holder. `CheckHealthAsync` (`:28`) gets the connection with the probe token (`:36`), opens a channel (`:38`), disposes it (`:39`) and returns `Healthy` (`:41`). A cancellation **of the probe token** is rethrown (`:43-46`); any other exception, including the holder's `ObjectDisposedException` after host shutdown or its `TimeoutException`, becomes a result with the registration's `FailureStatus` and the description `"RabbitMQ did not open a channel."` (`:48-55`).
- **Why it's built this way**: The check never opens or disposes a connection (`:9-12`): that is the holder's job, so a probe round leaves nothing behind on the broker. Opening a channel rather than only checking `IsOpen` makes the broker answer a real AMQP request. Tagged optional rather than gating readiness, because the broker is optional per host (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.Health.cs:63-64`): an outage degrades `/health` and stays off `/health/ready` (`:46-47`). **[ADR-009](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/009-resilience-and-recovery-objectives.html)** is the governing resilience record.
- **Where it's used**: Registered in `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` when a `rabbitmq` (or, failing that, `messaging`) connection string parses as an absolute URI (`Extensions.Health.cs:103-106`): the `HealthCheckRegistration` named `"rabbitmq"` constructs a check over the singleton holder (`:118-123`), tagged `HealthCheckTags.Optional` and given the per-check timeout from [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions). Covered by `DependencyHealthCheckTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Tests/Health/DependencyHealthCheckTests.cs:110`, `:129`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: A connection string that is not an absolute URI registers no RabbitMQ check at all, silently (`Extensions.Health.cs:105-106`). The channel is disposed only on the success path; if `CreateChannelAsync` throws there is no channel to dispose.

### RedisPingHealthCheck

> MMCA.Common.Aspire · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Health` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Health/RedisPingHealthCheck.cs:27` · Level 1 · class (internal, sealed)

- **What it is**: The framework's own Redis reachability check. It issues `PING` and nothing else, and reports the round-trip latency as its healthy description.
- **Depends on**: `StackExchange.Redis` (`IConnectionMultiplexer`, `ConnectionMultiplexer`, `ConfigurationOptions`), [RedisConfigurationOptionsPipeline](#redisconfigurationoptionspipeline) (for its owned connection), `IHealthCheck` / `HealthCheckResult` (ASP.NET Core diagnostics), and `IServiceProvider`. It pairs with [RedisCachingExtensions](#rediscachingextensions)' `AddRedisCaching()` and is registered by the [Common.Aspire Extensions](#extensions-1) under the [HealthCheckTags](#healthchecktags) `Optional` tag.
- **Concept introduced, a health check must not ask a question only an administrator can answer.** `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`, `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`, `[Rubric §11, Security]`. The class doc (`RedisPingHealthCheck.cs:9-26`) records a real incident shape. The `AspNetCore.HealthChecks.Redis` check this replaced branches on the detected server type and issues `CLUSTER INFO` against anything it considers clustered. Azure Managed Redis (Enterprise tier, port 10000) is detected as a cluster by StackExchange.Redis 3.x and refuses administrative commands unless the client opted into admin mode, so that check threw `RedisCommandException` on **every** probe against a perfectly healthy cache. The lesson generalises: a command whose success depends on the caller's privileges reports the caller as much as the dependency, and a probe cannot tell those two answers apart. `PING` answers the only question a health check has, "can this process talk to Redis right now", and is never gated on admin mode, on server topology, or on deployment tier. **[ADR-025](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/025-startup-warmup-readiness.html)** states the rule and names this file as its implementation.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - Four fields (`:29-32`): the connection string, the provider, a `SemaphoreSlim(1, 1)` connect gate, and a nullable `_ownedMultiplexer`. The constructor (`:34-38`) stores the first two and does no I/O, which is what lets the check be registered without a reachable server.
  - `CheckHealthAsync` (`:40`) null-guards the context (`:44`), then resolves the multiplexer with `_services.GetService<IConnectionMultiplexer>() ?? await GetOrCreateOwnedMultiplexerAsync()` (`:48-49`). That single line is the two-mode design in the doc comment (`:20-25`): a host that wired Redis through `AddRedisCaching()` shares the container's multiplexer, and a host that configured a connection string without registering a client gets one this check owns.
  - It then checks cancellation explicitly (`:51`), issues `multiplexer.GetDatabase().PingAsync()` (`:53`), and returns `Healthy` with the latency formatted invariantly (`:55-59`).
  - Two catch clauses in a deliberate order. `OperationCanceledException` **when the token was cancelled** is rethrown (`:61-64`), so host shutdown is not reported as a dependency fault. Everything else is reported, never thrown (`:66-75`, with the `CA1031` suppression scoped to that one clause at `:65` and `:67`), and the comment states the reason (`:69-70`): this check is tagged optional and must degrade the `/health` payload rather than fault the probe pipeline.
  - `DisposeAsync` (`:78-87`) disposes the owned multiplexer if one was created and always disposes the gate. `GetOrCreateOwnedMultiplexerAsync` (`:93-114`) connects under the semaphore only while `_ownedMultiplexer` is null (`:98`). It first asks `RedisConfigurationOptionsPipeline.CreateConfigured(_services, _connectionString)` (`:102`) for the options a registered Redis configurator produces: with none it connects from the connection string alone, otherwise from those configured options (`:103-105`), so the owned connection is configured the same way the DI client is (`:100-101`). The comment above the method (`:89-92`) is worth reading: there is no lock-free fast path on purpose, because probes run on a timer rather than the request path, and a failed connect leaves the field null so the next probe retries instead of latching a faulted result forever.
- **Why it's built this way**: `internal sealed` framework code rather than a per-host registration, because ADR-025 makes the probe command itself a framework-owned decision: a host that reaches for an Aspire client integration directly puts the package's untagged check back and defeats the rule. `IAsyncDisposable` rather than a fire-and-forget connection, so the fallback multiplexer is built once and released with the container. The owned path goes through the configurator pipeline for the same reason [PostgreSqlHealthCheck](#postgresqlhealthcheck) probes the shared data source: a probe that connects differently from the workload reports the probe, not the dependency. `PING` returns a `TimeSpan`, which is why the healthy description can carry latency at no extra cost.
- **Where it's used**: Registered inside `AddInfrastructureHealthChecks` only when a `redis` connection string is present (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Extensions.Health.cs:83-101`): `TryAddSingleton` builds it once (`:92-93`) and the `HealthCheckRegistration` named `"redis"` resolves that singleton, tags it `HealthCheckTags.Optional` and gives it the per-check timeout from [HealthReportCacheOptions](#healthreportcacheoptions) (`:95-100`), so a cache outage degrades `/health` without ever failing `/health/ready`. The Aspire client integrations' own checks are switched off at the source in [RedisCachingExtensions](#rediscachingextensions) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Caching/RedisCachingExtensions.cs:76-77`, `DisableHealthChecks = true` on both the distributed cache and the client) precisely so this one is the only Redis check in the pipeline. Covered by [RedisPingHealthCheckTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), whose cases include `CheckHealthAsync_UsesPingOnly_AndNeverAnAdministrativeCommand` and `CheckHealthAsync_WhenTheServerRefusesACommand_ReportsUnhealthyWithoutRethrowing`, and by [RedisReadinessSafetyTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) for the tagging.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `PING` proves reachability, not capacity: a cache that answers pings while evicting under memory pressure reports healthy. `PingAsync` takes no cancellation token, so a slow ping is bounded by the registration timeout and the StackExchange.Redis client timeouts, not by the explicit check at `:51`. Not determinable from source: which Redis configurators a consumer host registers.

### BrokerSelection

> MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting/BrokerSelection.cs:25` · Level 0 · class (public static)

- **What it is**: The framework's AppHost broker selector. It reads one app-named environment variable, provisions the matching broker once (`AddSelectedBroker`), and hands back an attach delegate that every service applies with `WithSelectedBroker`, so each service's wiring chain reads the same single line whichever broker is running.
- **Depends on**: `Aspire.Hosting` (`IDistributedApplicationBuilder`, `IResourceBuilder<DotnetProjectResource>`, `SqlServerServerResource`) and the Common AppHost-side [Extensions](#extensions-1): `AddMessageBroker` (`Extensions.cs:220`, RabbitMQ), `AddServiceBusEmulatorBroker` (`Extensions.cs:261`, returning a [ServiceBusEmulatorResource](#servicebusemulatorresource) builder) and the two `WithBroker` overloads (`Extensions.cs:312`, `:341`). The env var those calls ultimately set is read by [MessageBusSettings](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebussettings) / [MessageBusProviders](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebusproviders) in the consuming service.
- **Concept introduced, deferring a resource-type choice as a delegate.** `[Rubric §33, Developer Experience]`, `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]`, `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]`. The two `WithBroker` overloads take *different resource types*, so the choice cannot be one variable handed to one call. The class doc comment (`BrokerSelection.cs:8-20`) states the answer: `AddSelectedBroker` makes the choice once and returns it as a `Func<IResourceBuilder<DotnetProjectResource>, IResourceBuilder<DotnetProjectResource>>`, and `WithSelectedBroker` applies it, which stops a new service from quietly being wired to the wrong broker. §33 is the environment-parity category, and this type makes parity a one-variable switch instead of a per-service edit.
- **Walkthrough**: `IsServiceBusSelected(environmentVariableName)` (`:36`) rejects a blank name with `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` (`:38`) and returns true only when the variable equals `ServiceBusSelection`, the constant `"servicebus"` (`:28`), compared ordinal and case-insensitive (`:40-43`); unset or any other value is false. `AddSelectedBroker(environmentVariableName, sqlServer)` (`:58-60`, inside the `extension(IDistributedApplicationBuilder builder)` block at `:46`) null-guards `builder` and `sqlServer` (`:62-63`), then branches (`:65`): when selected it calls `builder.AddServiceBusEmulatorBroker(sqlServer)` and returns `service => service.WithBroker(serviceBus)` (`:67-68`); otherwise it calls `builder.AddMessageBroker().WithLifetime(ContainerLifetime.Persistent)` (a persistent container, so the broker survives an AppHost restart) and returns `service => service.WithBroker(rabbit)` (`:71-73`). `WithSelectedBroker(attach)` (`:82-83`, in the `extension(IResourceBuilder<DotnetProjectResource> service)` block at `:77`) null-guards the delegate (`:85`) and returns `attach(service)` (`:86`). The class carries a `CA1708` suppression with a justification (`:21-24`): with several `extension(T)` blocks in one static class the analyzer flags compiler-generated grouping members, a false positive.
- **Why it's built this way**: The selector lives in Common and the variable's name stays in the app, so each AppHost passes its own selector (`ADC_BROKER`) and keeps everything else about its stack. RabbitMQ is a different broker from the one production runs, so anything that only misbehaves on Azure Service Bus (entity-name limits, topic and subscription provisioning, scheduled redelivery pacing) is invisible in the inner loop. Opting in swaps in the official emulator, which reuses the already-declared `sqlServer` resource rather than starting a second engine. Unset stays on RabbitMQ because the emulator costs a second container plus a warm-up, "which is not a price the everyday inner loop should pay" (`:11-12`). The emulator's quota and TTL handling lives inside MMCA.Common's `AddBrokerMessaging`, keyed off the `UseDevelopmentEmulator=true` connection-string marker, so no service `Program.cs` changes. The transport-selection policy is **[ADR-066](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/066-broker-transport-selection.html)**; the `extension(T)` public surface follows **[ADR-106](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/106-extension-members-as-public-di-surface.html)**.
- **Where it's used**: ADC's AppHost declares `var withBroker = builder.AddSelectedBroker("ADC_BROKER", sqlServer);` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosting/MMCA.ADC.AppHost/Program.cs:98`) and hands that delegate to [ServiceInfrastructureExtensions](#serviceinfrastructureextensions)' `WithMmcaServiceInfrastructure` once per service, which applies `.WithSelectedBroker(attachBroker)` itself (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting/ServiceInfrastructureExtensions.cs:76`): Notification (`Program.cs:137-138`), Engagement (`:159`), Conference (`:197`) and Identity (`:222`). Store does the same with `AddSelectedBroker("STORE_BROKER", sqlServer)` (`MMCA.Store/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Store.AppHost/Program.cs:74`). `BrokerSelectionTests.cs` in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting.Tests/` covers it.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: When the broker env var is absent entirely (for example integration tests that boot a service through `WebApplicationFactory<Program>` with no AppHost), `AddBrokerMessaging` short-circuits to in-process mode and [InProcessMessageBus](group-04-events-outbox.md#inprocessmessagebus) keeps working (`Program.cs:64-67`); that fallback lives in the consuming service, not here.

### H2cHealthCheckRegistry

> MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting · `MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting/H2cHealthCheckExtensions.cs:154` · Level 0 · class (sealed, internal)

- **What it is**: The AppHost-tier twin of [GatewayDownstreamRegistry](#gatewaydownstreamregistry): a registration-time ledger of the resource-and-endpoint pairs already wired by `WithH2cHealthCheck`, so a repeated call cannot register a duplicate health-check key.
- **Depends on**: `IServiceCollection` and `StringComparer` (BCL). Used only by [H2cHealthCheckExtensions](#h2chealthcheckextensions).
- **Concept reinforced**: the registration-time ledger, taught at [GatewayDownstreamRegistry](#gatewaydownstreamregistry). `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]`, `[Rubric §14, Testability]`. The one idea this copy adds is in its own class doc (`H2cHealthCheckExtensions.cs:148-152`): the ledger is attached to the **AppHost's own service collection** rather than kept in a static field, so two `DistributedApplication.CreateBuilder` instances in one process, which is exactly what a test run is, never see each other's claims. A static ledger would make the second test in a file silently skip its registration.
- **Walkthrough**: `private readonly List<string> _keys = []` (`:160`), with the same list-over-`HashSet` note the gateway registry carries (`:155-159`). `TryClaim(key)` (`:168`) compares case-insensitively and returns `false` on a repeat (`:170-173`), otherwise appends and returns `true` (`:175-176`). `GetOrAdd(services)` (`:185`) scans the descriptors for a matching `ImplementationInstance` (`:187-194`) and otherwise registers a fresh one as a singleton (`:196-198`). The keys it holds are built by `CheckKey(resourceName, endpointName)` (`:75-76`), so the endpoint name is part of the identity and one resource can gate two endpoints.
- **Why it's built this way**: `internal sealed`, reachable from its tests through `<InternalsVisibleTo Include="MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting.Tests" />` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting.csproj:7`). It is a deliberate duplicate rather than a shared type: this assembly is the AppHost tier and must not reference the service-defaults assembly (see [Extensions](#extensions-1) for the same packaging rule applied to configuration section names).
- **Where it's used**: `WithH2cHealthCheck` claims its key before registering anything (`H2cHealthCheckExtensions.cs:122-125`). Both behaviours it buys are pinned by [H2cHealthCheckExtensionsTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup): `WithH2cHealthCheck_IsIdempotent`, `WithH2cHealthCheck_KeysTheCheckByEndpointSoOneResourceCanGateTwo`, and `WithH2cHealthCheck_DoesNotShareItsDuplicateGuardAcrossBuilders`.
