### GatewayTraceHeaderTransformProvider

> MMCA.Common.Gateway · `MMCA.Common.Gateway.Transforms` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gateway/Transforms/GatewayTraceHeaderTransformProvider.cs:18` · Level 3 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: The gateway kit's one request transform. It stamps two headers onto every proxied request, `X-MMCA-Route` with the route id YARP matched and `X-MMCA-Cluster` with the cluster it selected, after first removing whatever the client sent under those names.
- **Depends on**: [GatewaySettings](#gatewaysettings), injected as `IOptions<GatewaySettings>` and reduced immediately to its [GatewayTraceHeaderSettings](#gatewaytraceheadersettings) sub-object (`GatewayTraceHeaderTransformProvider.cs:18-21`). Externals: YARP's `ITransformProvider`, `TransformBuilderContext`, `TransformRouteValidationContext` / `TransformClusterValidationContext` and the `AddRequestTransform` extension (`Yarp.ReverseProxy.Transforms` / `.Transforms.Builder`, `:1-3`), plus `Microsoft.Extensions.Options`. Registered by [GatewayReverseProxyExtensions](#gatewayreverseproxyextensions) alongside [GatewayClusterProfileConfigFilter](#gatewayclusterprofileconfigfilter) and [GatewayHealthCheckDefaultsConfigFilter](#gatewayhealthcheckdefaultsconfigfilter).
- **Concept introduced, a header a downstream can trust.** `[Rubric §11, Security]`, `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`, `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]`. There are two ideas here and the second is the one that matters. The first is a YARP extension point: an `ITransformProvider` is asked to contribute transforms into a route's pipeline when that pipeline is built, which is how a gateway adds behaviour to *every* route without listing it in each route's JSON. The second is the trust question. Correlating a downstream log line back to a gateway route by matching URL path patterns stops working the moment two routes share a prefix, so the proxy stamps the answer it alone knows. But the instant a downstream *trusts* a header, that header becomes an input an attacker can supply: a client that sets `X-MMCA-Route: identity-auth` itself would have its request attributed to a route it never matched. §11 is the category that cares, and the fix is structural rather than validating: the transform **removes both header names before writing its own** (`:60-61`), so the value a service reads can only have come from the gateway. The class doc states the rule directly, "A header a service trusts must be one only the gateway can set" (`:11-15`). §7 is why the fact is worth having at all: once modules run as separate deployables ([ADR-008](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/008-service-extraction-topology.html)), route selection is a decision made in one process and consumed in the logs of another.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - A primary constructor takes `IOptions<GatewaySettings>` and the single field `_settings` is initialized to `options.Value.TraceHeaders` with an inline `ArgumentNullException` guard (`:18-21`). The provider therefore holds the trace-header sub-settings only, resolved once at construction: the DI registration supplies `IOptions<GatewaySettings>` as a fixed `Options.Create(settings)` singleton (`GatewayReverseProxyExtensions.cs:90`), so there is no reload path to honour.
  - `ValidateRoute` (`:25`) and `ValidateCluster` (`:33`) are deliberately empty, each with a comment saying why (`:27-28`, `:35`): the provider is applied to every route unconditionally and reads nothing from the route's own transform list, so there is no operator-supplied parameter that could be mistyped and therefore nothing to validate. YARP requires the two methods; implementing them as no-ops with a stated reason is the honest form.
  - `Apply(TransformBuilderContext context)` (`:40`) null-guards the context (`:42`), then applies the **kill switch**: when `_settings.Enabled` is false it returns having added nothing (`:44-47`). Disabled means no transform in the pipeline at all, not a transform that runs and does nothing.
  - Four values are hoisted into locals *before* the transform delegate is created: the two configured header names and the route's `RouteId` and `ClusterId` (`:51-54`). The comment above them (`:49-50`) is the rationale: these ids are fixed for the lifetime of this route's transform pipeline, so resolving them per request would be work that can never produce a different answer. The captured locals are what keep the per-request delegate to a few header operations.
  - `context.AddRequestTransform(...)` (`:56`) registers the delegate. Inside it: take `transformContext.ProxyRequest.Headers` (`:58`), `Remove` both header names (`:60-61`), then `TryAddWithoutValidation` each id back **only when it is non-empty** (`:63-71`), and return `ValueTask.CompletedTask` (`:73`). The empty checks are not defensive noise: a direct-response route has no cluster, and the behaviour there is to stamp the route header and leave the cluster header absent rather than emit a blank one.
- **Why it's built this way**: Remove-then-add rather than "set" is the whole security property, and doing it inside the *request* transform (which shapes the outbound message to the destination) rather than in middleware is what guarantees no later stage re-copies the inbound value. `TryAddWithoutValidation` skips the header-syntax checks, which matters because route and cluster ids come from operator-authored configuration and a strict `Add` would throw inside the proxy pipeline for an id containing an unexpected character. This transform is also a deliberate, documented exception to the gateway kit's own charter: **[ADR-088](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/088-gateway-edge-responsibilities.html)** originally declined all request rewriting and was narrowed on 2026-08-27 to allow exactly this one, on the grounds that it stamps *which route and cluster YARP selected*, "a fact only the proxy knows and one a downstream cannot reconstruct". The declines that still stand are path rewriting, body rewriting and response shaping.
- **Where it's used**: Registered once, in the private `Wire` body shared by both `AddMmcaGateway` overloads, as `.AddTransforms<GatewayTraceHeaderTransformProvider>()` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gateway/GatewayReverseProxyExtensions.cs:103`). Both gateway hosts pick it up through that one call: `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:173` and `MMCA.Store/Source/Hosts/MMCA.Store.Gateway/Program.cs:140`. Covered directly by [GatewayTraceHeaderTransformProviderTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), whose five cases pin exactly the behaviours above: the happy path (`GatewayTraceHeaderTransformProviderTests.cs:18`), that a client-supplied value is replaced rather than appended to (`:29`, asserting a single header value at `:39-42`), custom header names (`:46`), that `Enabled = false` leaves `context.RequestTransforms` empty (`:65-75`), and the cluster-less route (`:81-88`). Its presence in the registration is asserted by [AddMmcaGatewayTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) (`AddMmcaGatewayTests.cs:39`), and [RecordingHttpForwarderTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) models the same stamping in its test double (`RecordingHttpForwarderTests.cs:159-169`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: Neither app's configuration overrides the `MmcaGateway:TraceHeaders` section, so the shipped names are the defaults `X-MMCA-Route` and `X-MMCA-Cluster` (`GatewaySettings.cs:210`, `:184`) and `Enabled` stays true (`GatewaySettings.cs:216`). Nothing in this file consumes the headers: what a downstream does with them is out of scope here, and source shows no first-party reader of either name. The settings snapshot is taken in the constructor and the `Enabled` gate is evaluated when a route's transform pipeline is built, so a configuration change requires a new proxy configuration build, not merely the next request.

### AdcOutageCollection

> MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests · `MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests` · `MMCA.ADC/Tests/Integration/MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests/AdcOutageCollection.cs:9` · Level 4 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the xUnit collection definition that shares one [AdcOutageAppHostFixture](#adcoutageapphostfixture) across the outage tests, with parallelization off (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Integration/MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests/AdcOutageCollection.cs:8-9`).
- **Depends on**: xUnit's `ICollectionFixture<T>` and `[CollectionDefinition]`, closed over [AdcOutageAppHostFixture](#adcoutageapphostfixture).
- **Concept introduced, serialize what shares a breakable dependency.** `[Rubric §14, Testability]`. An outage test stops a container the whole stack shares, so nothing else may run against the AppHost while a dependency is down (`:3-7`). `DisableParallelization = true` (`:8`) is what turns that rule into a guarantee rather than a hope.
- **Walkthrough**: `[CollectionDefinition(Name, DisableParallelization = true)]` (`:8`) on an empty sealed class implementing `ICollectionFixture<AdcOutageAppHostFixture>` (`:9`), with one constant, `Name = "adc-outage"` (`:12`), that test classes join with `[Collection]`.
- **Why it's built this way**: a named constant rather than a repeated string keeps the definition and every `[Collection]` attribute in step at compile time. One fixture per collection means the AppHost boots once for the whole tier, which matters given its twelve-minute start budget.
- **Where it's used**: `[Collection(AdcOutageCollection.Name)]` on [AdcDependencyOutageTests](#adcdependencyoutagetests) (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Integration/MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests/AdcDependencyOutageTests.cs:21`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: none beyond the fixture's own preconditions.

### AdcDependencyOutageTests

> MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests · `MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests` · `MMCA.ADC/Tests/Integration/MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests/AdcDependencyOutageTests.cs:22` · Level 5 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: ADC's dependency-outage tier: two scenarios that stop a real container under the running ADC stack and assert what Notification's probes answer while it is down and after it returns (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Integration/MMCA.ADC.AppHost.OutageTests/AdcDependencyOutageTests.cs:7-13`).
- **Depends on**: the shipped [DependencyOutageTestBase<TFixture>](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) (`:22-23`) with [DependencyOutageScenario](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) and [DependencyOutageKind](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) from `MMCA.Common.Testing.Aspire.Outages`; [AdcOutageAppHostFixture](#adcoutageapphostfixture) through [AdcOutageCollection](#adcoutagecollection) (`:21`); AwesomeAssertions.
- **Concept introduced, degradation as an asserted contract.** `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`, `[Rubric §14, Testability]`. Each scenario opens with the base's baseline (both probes 200), stops one container, and asserts that a cache or broker outage fails health but keeps readiness, because those checks are tagged optional, then starts it again and asserts recovery (`:8-12`). The broker scenario adds the outbox half of the story: an event raised during the outage must be parked, then drained once the broker is back. The remarks record the deliberate gap: there is no SQL Server outage scenario, because an Aspire start recreates a stopped container with empty databases and the services migrate only at start, so a "recovered" readiness would be asserted on services whose schema is gone (`:14-18`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - `RedisOutage_KeepsReadiness_FailsHealth` (`:25-42`): skips with the fixture's reason when it is unavailable (`:28-31`), then runs a `Cache` scenario against `redis`, probing `notification` on its `http` endpoint (`:33-39`).
  - `BrokerOutage_KeepsReadiness_AndIdentityOutboxDrains` (`:44-63`): a `Broker` scenario against `rabbitmq`, with `OutboxDatabaseResourceName` set to `adc-identity` and `DuringOutage = RegisterOneAttendeeAsync` (`:52-60`).
  - `RegisterOneAttendeeAsync` (`:73-97`): a client on the gateway's `https` endpoint (`:75-77`) posts a fresh registration to `/Auth/register` with `api-version: 1.0` (`:79-90`) and asserts `201 Created`, because registration writes to Identity's database, which is up during a broker outage (`:94-96`). That commit parks a `UserRegistered` event in Identity's outbox, which the base asserts pending and then drained (`:65-70`). The body is the shape the conference-day load test sends (`:68-69`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the scenarios are data handed to a shipped base, so the orchestration (baseline, stop, probe, start, recover) lives once in Common and the ADC class only says which container, which service and what to do mid-outage. Going through the gateway for the registration exercises the real edge path rather than calling Identity directly.
- **Where it's used**: test class; no references outside the defining file.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: opt-in and container-dependent through its fixture, so it skips in a default run. Not determinable from source: how often CI runs this tier.
